### RH as reflection, torsion and ordinate injectivity
- MUST use a regularization whose entrywise multiplier is a positive kernel: the raw prime-side moment blocks are PSD at every finite aperture with no hypothesis (their unregularized limit is a nonnegative form on finitely supported vectors); an entrywise multiplier preserves PSD inputs iff it is PSD (Schur; all-ones block). The factorial regularization is not one: `1/(m+n)!` = diagonal congruence × `1/binom(m+n,m)` (2×2 corner determinant `−1/2`; no congruence fixes it since `c_mc_n=1/(m+n)!` is contradictory at indices 0,1); first trace block multiplier `[[1,1/6],[1/6,1/120]]` (determinant `−7/360`) breaks even the all-ones block ⇒ the loss of positivity there is a property of the normalization, not of the source. The reciprocal factorials are on the wrong side of Cauchy–Schwarz at every off-diagonal pair (`((m+n)!)²<(2m)!(2n)!` for `m≠n`; overshoot `√((2m)!(2n)!)/(m+n)!` exponential) ⇒ the free raw positivity is anti-related to the Jensen criterion at every scale; admissible normalizations are congruences, Hadamard powers and limits of positive kernels (the rank-one `1/√((2m)!(2n)!)` is fine). (MUST-391r, CANNOT-392r)
- CANNOT obtain the needed reflection positivity from the free translation positivity: prime-side Gram matrices are Toeplitz (translation-covariant explicit formula), zero criteria need Hankel (FE is a reflection); for one frequency `Σu_mu_ncos((m−n)x)=C²+S²` (always ≥0) but `Σu_mu_ncos((m+n)x)=C²−S²` is a comparison (`−1` for one atom at `x=π/2` with weight on index 1). On the multiplicative group of positive reals the reflection kernel = translation kernel with one argument inverted; the group is torsion free, so the reflection kernel's diagonal is `Φ` on squares, negative at arbitrarily large arguments unconditionally ⇒ not PSD; under RH its antipodal two-point form `2Φ(square)−2Φ(0)` is nonpositive everywhere ⇒ reflection positivity on this group is false, not missing. (MUST-393r, CANNOT-397)
- MUST run any reflection-positivity argument on a carrier whose inversion has a large fixed set (abundant two-torsion), strictly between the divisor group and its square-class quotient, retaining the valuation grading: the principal divisors of ℤ are free abelian (no two-torsion); ℚ gets exactly one nontrivial involution (the sign), at the archimedean place — which is why the prime and archimedean organs are sampling-disjoint and prime data alone never produced the comparison, and why the scalar level-one symmetry never carried zero information; congruence quotients with `r` prime factors have fixed sets of size `2^r` (FE = inversion of reduced residues, Fourier matrix flat away from one row); the square-class quotient makes inversion trivial and kills the condition. (MUST-398)
- CANNOT detect RH with any real-valued reflection-even reader: RH = the Hermitian reflection `ρ↦1−ρ̄` (fixing exactly the critical line) acts trivially on the divisor, i.e. the permutation module of divisor copies has no free orbit; free orbits come in pairs ⇒ the separating datum is two-torsion; the horizontal grade `2Re ρ−1` is reflection-odd; every norm, energy, squared amplitude, signature, Gram determinant, index, capacity, positivity takes the same value on a copy and its partner, and the canonical even coordinate (grade²) is consistent with any off-line copy ⇒ the universal map from a two-torsion group to ℝ is zero — the structural cause of the record that every audited operation transports, forgets or repackages the anomaly. (CANNOT-399)
- MUST read four obstructions as one `𝔽₂`-valued class: (1) the reflection kernel = translation kernel ∘ inversion with point fixed locus on the torsion-free divisor group; (2) dually, inversion fixes all quadratic characters while zeros sit on the archimedean line, transverse to that locus (rational independence of prime logs) ⇒ the reflection form is metabolic (one hyperbolic plane per conjugate pair, Witt class 0); (3) twin rigidity (every factorization is a square root dressed by a unit; the ladder's open core is a half-divisor); (4) the dyadic-root tower and the global parity wall (odd-order critical zeros above every height). The squaring map on characters has kernel the quadratic characters and no continuous section already at one prime. The one exact machine computing a global two-torsion class from local ramification is genus theory (ambiguous class number formula). (MUST-400)
- MUST read RH as ordinate injectivity: the Hermitian reflection preserves the imaginary part ⇒ the action is fibred over height; each horizontal slice is finite (divisor locally finite in the strip) and reflection-stable; a free orbit = two distinct zeros at exactly the same ordinate ⇒ RH ⇔ distinct divisor points have distinct ordinates ⇔ every slice has at most one point ⇔ every left half-slice is empty. Any height with a single divisor point is already critical, unconditionally; the content sits on heights carrying ≥2 points (at most `C log` height of them). Over `𝔽₂` the Galois norm of the reflection squares to zero and its Tate cohomology is Chevalley's ⇒ RH ⇔ that norm vanishes identically on the mod-2 divisor module (no estimate, positivity or energy). (MUST-401, MUST-403, MUST-404, MUST-408)
- MUST distinguish excluding a small displacement (resolution: pair statistics resolve real parts only at window×distance; fixed-length mollifiers give density exponent `2θ`; Gaussian widths certifiable only at the trivial edge — all stop one power of log short) from excluding an exact coincidence of two ordinates (a codimension/transversality condition: argument-principle counts, derivative non-vanishing, genericity of a projection — scale-insensitive and never attempted). (CANNOT-402)
- CANNOT decide RH from the fixed (ambiguous) part or any localized invariant: on each slice the fixed part is a subsingleton unconditionally (the critical point at that height), so over a window the genus invariant is a raw count of critical zeros (the walled Hardy–Selberg–Levinson–Conrey counting problem); slice count = fixed bit + 2·(free orbits). For a `ℤ/2` action on a finite set Borel equivariant cohomology is a polynomial ring at fixed points and the ground field at free orbits ⇒ free orbits are torsion for the equivariant parameter, and inverting it (Tate cohomology, Smith theory, signatures, Fredholm/Toeplitz indices, Krein inertia, Witt/Hasse classes, spectral flow, Maslov index) kills them by construction (Atiyah–Segal–Quillen) ⇒ every localized invariant computes only the fixed part. Chevalley/genus/ambiguous-class formulas compute the fixed part and quotient away the free part; no machine computing a free-orbit count from local data is known. MUST target an unlocalized equivariant statement: the action is equivariantly formal (free part empty). (CANNOT-405, MUST-406, CANNOT-407)
- MUST compute what an imported machine outputs in the target setting before developing the analogy: a machine whose output is already known or fixed independently of the question is a receiver however well its hypotheses match (the ambiguous-class route passed every structural check and died because its output is one bit per height). (MUST-409)

### Criticality, parity and transverse equilibrium
- MUST read RH as unbroken parity: among the involutions preserving the divisor, conjugation (reverses ordinate) = time reversal, the FE (reverses both) = PT, the Hermitian reflection (fixes ordinate, reflects real part) = parity ⇒ RH ⇔ parity unbroken ⇔ time (the ordinate) is a complete observable on the divisor; failure = a parity doublet at one instant. Unconditionally: the number of zeros at an instant is odd exactly when the critical point at that height is a zero. (MUST-410)
- CANNOT expect any inequality to carry slack: the de Bruijn–Newman threshold is `≥0` unconditionally and `≤0` exactly under RH ⇒ RH says the threshold is exactly 0 — the configuration sits on the boundary between broken and unbroken parity (the time needed to make the action formal is 0, with none to spare). This single fact explains the recurring pattern: every certified estimate stops one log power short, no profile improves it, certified widths are exactly the trivial ones, the exponent is `½` unconditionally and `0` under RH with nothing between, positivity is marginal with no spectral gap, every prime power is load-bearing ⇒ MUST aim at an exact marginality identity (vanishing of a derivative), not a bound. (CANNOT-411)
- MUST know the marginality identity and its closed form: taking real parts in the Calogero flow, the displacement `Re ρ−½` evolves by the graph heat equation on the zero graph with conductances `1/|ρ−ρ'|²` ⇒ RH is an equilibrium: the transverse Coulomb force `Σ_{ρ'≠ρ}(Re ρ−Re ρ')/|ρ−ρ'|²` vanishes at every zero (Euler–Lagrange equation of the transverse Dirichlet energy the flow dissipates); at an off-line zero the partner's term is `1/(2·displacement)`, diverging as displacement → 0. Closed form at a simple zero (Hadamard partial fractions): `ξ''(ρ)/ξ'(ρ)` is purely imaginary ⇔ every zero is a horizontal critical point of `|ξ'|`; unconditionally [L] `ξ'` changes sign under the point reflection through the strip centre, so `|ξ'|` is reflection-invariant and `ξ'` vanishes at the centre. [L] The zero network is locally finite (total conductance at every vertex converges) and the force is absolutely convergent. (MUST-412, MUST-414, MUST-416)
- CANNOT derive the marginality identity variationally: identities "sum over other zeros of reciprocal separations = fixed function at the zero" come from second-order linear ODEs (Stieltjes electrostatics: the configuration is the critical point of an explicit energy), but `ξ` is differentially transcendental, even after adjoining any finite bath of auxiliary functions ⇒ that mechanism does not exist here. (CANNOT-415)
- MUST know the chain reduces to the identity + attainment: inputs of the Liouville chain are separable — harmonicity (the identity), boundedness (given by the zero-free region), recurrence (true but marginal: in the ordinate variable the conductance is the Cauchy jump kernel, stable index exactly 1 = recurrence/transience boundary; the cutset criterion is vacuous because the cut conductance is a divergent harmonic series; flow/Fourier criteria not in the library), and constancy ⇒ RH (proved: constant real part forces `½` by the FE). Recurrence is unnecessary: the network is complete (all conductances positive), so at a point of maximal real part every force term is `≥0`, the identity makes their sum 0, hence every term 0 ⇒ the identity + an attained maximal real part gives RH outright; the non-attained regime (supremum approached, never attained) is exactly where the maximum principle has no point — the same dichotomy as for single-base lags and window averages. (MUST-413, CANNOT-417, MUST-418, MUST-419)

### Critical-line potential, angular energy and signature
Notation: `δ=Re ρ−½`; angular energy of a zero = `Σ_{ρ′≠ρ}cos²θ`, `cos²θ=(Re ρ−Re ρ′)²/|ρ−ρ′|²`; critical-line potential `U(t)=Σ_ρδ²/(δ²+(t−Im ρ)²)`.
- MUST-420 — angular energy is quantized at one. `cos²θ = (Re ρ − Re ρ′)²/|ρ − ρ′|²`; the sum over other zeros converges. The functional-equation partner lies horizontally opposite at distance `|2 Re ρ − 1|`, so its term is exactly `1`, independent of the distance to the line. **Must:** angular energy `≥ 1` at every off-line zero, `≡ 0` under the hypothesis; values never lie entirely in `(0,1)`. **Cannot:** treat a near-line zero as a small perturbation of any Cauchy-kernel functional.
- MUST-421 — threshold form, attainment eliminated. Hypothesis ⟺ angular energy `< 1` at every zero ⟺ `= 0` at a single zero. **Must:** a threshold with a margin, unlike the rigid equivalents held here; any unconditional bound below one suffices. **Cannot:** count attainment of the supremum of the real part among the obligations — no extremal zero, no limiting configuration, no escape-to-infinity case is used.
- CANNOT-422 — no continuous deformation sees the quantum. The partner term is `1` off the line and `0` on it: the energy jumps, it does not decay. **Cannot:** detect the hypothesis by any functional continuous under a motion of the zeros. **Must:** the separating invariant is integer-valued and the flow is continuous.
- CANNOT-423 — the angular energy is not holomorphic. `cos²θ = ½ + ½ Re(Δ/Δ̄)`. **Cannot:** evaluate or bound it by Hadamard products, logarithmic derivatives or Taylor coefficients — those give `Σ Δ⁻²`, never `Σ Δ/Δ̄`. **Must:** the missing object is a non-holomorphic self-pairing; the recorded toolkit saturation is of the holomorphic half only.
- MUST-424 — the critical-line potential is a sum of unit bumps. `U(t) = Σ_ρ δ²/(δ² + (t − Im ρ)²)`, `δ = Re ρ − 1/2`, converges. Each summand is `1/(1 + ((t − Im ρ)/δ)²)`: height `1`, width `δ`; on-line zeros contribute nothing. **Must:** hypothesis ⟺ `U < 1` uniformly, one function of one real variable, no attainment. Superlevel set at height `λ` has half-width `δ√((1−λ)/λ)`: detecting displacement `δ` needs resolution `δ`.
- CANNOT-425 — mass blindness. Bump mass over half-width `R` is `2δ·arctan(R/δ) < πδ`; height is `1` for every `δ`. **Cannot:** decide the hypothesis by any criterion reading the divisor through a displacement mass `Σ|δ_ρ|` — `∫N(σ,T)dσ` is half that mass, Littlewood's lemma its logarithmic mean, mollified moments and smoothed counts the same kind. **Must:** require a supremum at unknown resolution, not an average at fixed resolution.
- MUST-426 — the discriminant law. Orbit quadratic `Q_ρ(s) = (s − ρ)(s − (1 − ρ̄))`; `disc = (2 Re ρ − 1)²`, constant; on the line `Q_ρ(1/2+it) = −[(Re ρ − 1/2)² + (t − Im ρ)²]`; the bump is `−disc(Q_ρ)/(4Q_ρ)`. **Must:** hypothesis ⟺ every orbit discriminant vanishes ⟺ every orbit quadratic is a perfect square; the potential is the generating function of the discriminants.
- MUST-427 — the threshold is two. The partner shares the ordinate and reflects the real part, so two zeros each contribute a full unit there. **Must:** `U ≥ 2` at the ordinate of any off-line zero; the hypothesis is the bound below two.
- CANNOT-428 — the weight trichotomy. A weight `δ²/(δ² + τ²)^k` receives `δ^{2−2k}` at the zero's own ordinate: `δ²`, `1`, `δ⁻²` at `k = 0,1,2`. **Cannot:** decide at either flanking exponent — the averaged family sits at the vanishing one, the Laguerre expression at the diverging one, where `−2/δ²` must cancel against an unbounded sum. **Must:** `k = 1` is the only surviving weight.
- MUST-429 — the matching is conjugation. In the spectral coordinate `z = (ρ − 1/2)(−i)` the partner is the complex conjugate. **Must:** the matching needs no choice — it is conjugate pairing of the non-real zeros of a real entire function, and each orbit quadratic is the corresponding real quadratic factor. **Cannot:** identify the obstruction with a half-divisor or a missing canonical square root; that identification is withdrawn.
- MUST-430 — the potential is a linear Blaschke defect. `B_ρ(t) = (t − z̄_ρ)/(t − z_ρ)` is unimodular on `ℝ`, `= 1` exactly on the line, partner's factor its conjugate. `bump = ‖1 − B_ρ‖²/4 = (1 − Re B_ρ)/2`, and `Σ_ρ(1 − B_ρ) = 2U`. **Must:** the potential is linear in the defects, and `1 − B_ρ(t) = 2i(Re ρ − 1/2)/(t − z_ρ)` — the resolvent weighted by the displacement. **Cannot:** obtain that weighting from holomorphic data, which give the unweighted power sums `Σ(t − z_ρ)^{-k}`, conjugation-symmetric.
- MUST-431 — localised Hermite positivity. At a real base point off the spectral divisor, `u_q = (t − z_q)^{-1}`; the power sums `p_k = Σ u_q^k` converge for `k ≥ 2`, and `p_2` is an explicit derivative of the logarithmic derivative. For a finite real test vector the Hankel form factors unconditionally as `Σ_{i,j} c_i c_j p_{i+j+2} = Σ_q P(u_q)²`. **Must:** under the hypothesis every `u_q` is real, so the form is a nonnegative real at every base point and test vector; one negative value refutes. **Cannot:** claim the converse — the finite Hermite–Sylvester converse does not transfer to an infinite divisor.
- MUST-432 — the monomial witness. At the ordinate of an off-line zero the resolvents of the zero and its partner are purely imaginary of modulus `1/δ`, and a purely imaginary number to an exponent `≡ 2 mod 4` is a negative real; the orbit contributes `−2/δ^{4n+2}`. Other zeros, of resolvent modulus `≤ 1/δ′` with `δ′ > δ`, carry `(1/δ′)^{4n}` against `(1/δ)^{4n}`. **Must:** an off-line zero strictly closer to the base point than any other is refuted by one evaluation of one derivative of the logarithmic derivative. **Cannot:** remove the isolation hypothesis; the inner band is the case `n = 0`, the member with no suppression.
- MUST-433 — the scaling degree law. `bump(cd, cg, ct) = bump(d, g, t)`: functionals are graded by homogeneity degree. The peak is degree zero; every pointwise average is degree one, the integral being the displacement times a profile integral. **Cannot:** reach the hypothesis with any pointwise-average functional — no finite `L^p`, mollified moment, smoothed count or nonlinear average. **Must:** the degree-zero class also holds the total variation and the count; conversion laws exist and are sharp, but each supplies a scale and the only scale is the displacement.
- MUST-434 — the decided invariant is a count. Every off-line point of an ordinate has its bump at full height there, so the free part of the slice counts itself inside the potential, with even cardinality, twice the left half-slice. **Must:** one free orbit forces `U ≥ 2`; the hypothesis is the vanishing of that count at every ordinate; the potential is an upper envelope of a count. **Must:** the deciding invariant is integer-valued while every analytic functional is continuous; the functional-equation class with arbitrary finite Euler heads admits both fixed-orbit and free-orbit extensions at a generic off-line point; and the involution is the functional equation composed with complex conjugation. **Cannot:** expect a producer manufacturing the zeros from the source.
- CANNOT-435 — coboundary, not cohomology. Over `𝔽₂`, `Nf = f + f∘σ`, `N∘N = 0`. The left half of the strip is a canonical section, so `N(f·1_{Re<1/2}) = f` for every invariant `f` vanishing on the fixed locus; the coboundary is surjective onto those, the Tate cohomology is the functions on the line alone, and `N(1_{Re<1/2}) = 1_{Re≠1/2}`. **Cannot:** obtain the hypothesis from any equivariant invariant, localisation or completion — all compute cohomology, and the hypothesis is the vanishing of a coboundary, which cohomology discards. **Must:** aim a producer at the boundary map.
- MUST-436 — the boundary map. A cochain is a coboundary iff it is invariant and vanishes on the line; the image is an explicit subspace, freely determined by one bit per free orbit. The involution preserves ordinates, so the map is fibred and its rank is the sum over ordinates of the free-orbit count. Over any finite ordinate set, total count = critical-line count + twice the free-orbit count. **Must:** the hypothesis is their agreement on every window — the classical `N` versus `N₀` relation in exact finite form, so this lane and the classical count decide the same integer, differing only in access: averaged there, fibrewise here.
- CANNOT-437 — the required bound is an absolute constant. The free-orbit count at an ordinate is at most the Cauchy-weighted local zero density there, of size a constant times the logarithm of the height. **Cannot:** use any estimate of that shape — the count is an integer and twice it is dominated by the potential, so a bound below two forces vanishing and a bound of two or more forces nothing. **Must:** the required estimate is an absolute constant, uniform in the height; the obstruction is height-dependence, not strength; integrality removes the slack.
- MUST-438 — a signature, not a bound. The hypothesis says an involution on a discrete set has no free orbits — over a base field, that an étale algebra is totally real. Such statements are decided by a trace form: the signature of `ℝ^r × ℂ^s` gives each complex pair one positive and one negative direction, so vanishing of the complex part is positive semidefiniteness and the free-orbit count is the negative index. **Must:** the target is a signature computation. The localised Hermite form is that trace form — each orbit one real direction, fixed orbits never negative, free orbits indefinite. **Must:** `sign(disc) = (−1)^s`, so a determinant sees only the parity: the parity law is the determinant of the form and the counting problem its signature.
- MUST-439 — the sign criterion is the Hermite–Biehler lane, already held. A real entire function has only real zeros iff its logarithmic derivative maps the upper half-plane into the lower. Termwise `−Im(z − z_ρ)^{-1} = (Im z − Im z_ρ)/|z − z_ρ|²`: on-line zeros contribute positively throughout, an off-line zero has an orbit member of positive spectral height whose contribution at half that height is `−2/Im z_ρ`. **Cannot:** treat this as a new route — it is the Hermite–Biehler lane, held in stronger form, where strict inequality for the carrier `XiC + i·XiC'` gives the hypothesis and the known gap is strictness. **Cannot:** claim harmonic positivity evades the other obstructions: the function is harmonic only away from the non-real zeros, and a maximum principle needs that unknown set.
- MUST-440 — the target sits at the critical Sobolev exponent. `‖bump‖_p^p = c_p·d`, so `‖bump‖_p ∝ d^{1/p}`: `p = ∞` is the only scale-invariant Lebesgue exponent. The transform is `π d·e^{−d|ξ|}`, so `‖bump‖²_{H^s} ≍ d^{1−2s}`: `s = 1/2` is the only scale-invariant Sobolev exponent. **Must:** the degree-zero class holds both `H^{1/2}` and `L^∞`. **Cannot:** control the second by the first — in one dimension `H^s ↪ L^∞` needs `s > 1/2` strictly, and the family sits at the failing endpoint. **Must:** scale-invariance is necessary but not sufficient; the deficit is one logarithm, which the absolute-constant requirement forbids. This is the exponent `1/2` recurring in the interlacing window, the Riemann–Siegel main-sum length and the required Gibbs decay.
- CANNOT-441 — neither extreme-controlling mechanism survives. **Cannot** use a duality certificate: for the reflection cone `{Σ t_j cosh(w_j u) even real, ≥ 0}` the semidefinite dual is the moment side, so failure of sum-of-squares representability is a duality gap, and its cylinder-lift witness is degree-free. That cone **is** sums of Hermitian squares when all frequencies are real and when all are imaginary; only the mixed case fails, so the gap is the on-line/off-line mixture itself. **Cannot** use Dobrushin contraction: on the prime side parity moments factor as `∏(1 − p^m)`, so local influences amplify by `p^m`; on the divisor side the normalised influence matrix has row sums exactly one, failing with zero margin, because the marginality identity is an averaging identity. **Must:** positivity certifies `max ≤ total` while the total is the unknown; the surviving question is recurrence, not contraction.
- MUST-442 — off-line zeros are critical-exponent bubbles. The critical seminorm has the Gagliardo form `∫∫|f(x) − f(y)|²/|x − y|²`, and the zero network with conductances `1/|ρ_j − ρ_k|²` is its discretisation: the angular energy is the local critical energy. The bump has critical seminorm `π/4`, independent of `d`. **Must:** the partner's unit is energy quantisation; the bound below one is the statement that the configuration is below the first critical level; the absolute constant demanded is the first bubble energy; and in any finite collection of off-line zeros the cardinality is at most the total angular energy. **Cannot:** import the below-threshold technology, which constructs a competitor under the bubble level and so needs a variational problem whose critical points are the divisor.

### Pairwise zero dynamics (Calogero heat flow)
- MUST-443 — the pair field is a reciprocal product; the collision budget is bounded. The surrounding zeros enter the gap equation through `Δ = Σ_y[(x₁ − y)(x₂ − y)]⁻¹`, not inverse squares; it follows from `(x₂ − y)⁻¹ − (x₁ − y)⁻¹ = −(x₂ − x₁)[(x₁ − y)(x₂ − y)]⁻¹` and converts the Calogero velocity difference into `−4/δ + 2δΔ` exactly. Every term is positive outside the gap. **Must:** with `θ = Δu₀`, `2Δτ* = ½ log(2/(2−θ))`, bounded whenever a collision occurs, and over that interval the comparison field decays by `(1 + ½ log(2/(2−θ)))⁻¹ < 1`. **Cannot:** attribute loss to the frozen-field approximation — the remaining obligation is the transport term alone, over a bounded fraction of the damping timescale and against a quantified headwind.
- MUST-444 — the two energies coincide at an on-line zero. The angular energy of a zero weights by the squared difference of displacements; the critical-line potential weights by the squared displacement. At a zero of the critical line the base displacement is zero and the weights agree termwise, including on the diagonal, so `angularEnergy ρ₀ = U(Im ρ₀)` whenever `Re ρ₀ = 1/2`. **Must:** the two thresholds recorded for these objects are one threshold, and an on-line zero sharing its ordinate with an off-line zero carries angular energy at least two.
- MUST-445 — the transport term is removable; the residual obstruction is diffusion. Along a path of velocity `ż = −2L` the convective derivative cancels the transport term exactly: `d/dτ V(z(τ),τ) = V_zz − 2V²`. The characteristics are not auxiliary — `−2L(z) = −2Σ_k(z − x_k)⁻¹` is the Calogero velocity field, so the zeros travel along them and the field equation carries no transport in Lagrangian coordinates. **Cannot** therefore attribute the residual loss in a collision lemma to transport. **Must:** what remains is the diffusion, and its sign is adverse where it matters: between consecutive real zeros the field tends to `+∞` at both ends, so it has an interior minimum with `V_zz ≥ 0`. The comparison is not marginal — at the midpoint of a gap `δ` the pair's own part gives `V = 8/δ²`, `V_zz = 192/δ⁴`, `2V² = 128/δ⁴`, so diffusion exceeds damping by exactly `3/2` and the raw field grows along the characteristic. **Must** consequently run any collision lemma on the regular part alone: the pair's singular part is already exact in `u' = 4Δu − 8`, but the damping bound does not apply to the raw field near a close pair.
- MUST-446 — the pair field is a pointwise value of the regular field inside the gap. Partial fractions give `[(x₁ − y)(x₂ − y)]⁻¹ = [(x₁ − y)⁻¹ − (x₂ − y)⁻¹]/(x₂ − x₁)`, so the pair field of a consecutive pair is the divided difference `Δ = [L(x₁) − L(x₂)]/(x₂ − x₁)` of the regular logarithmic derivative across the gap. Consecutiveness places every surrounding zero outside the closed gap, so `L` is differentiable there with derivative minus the regular field, and the mean value theorem gives `Δ = V(ξ)` for some `ξ` strictly inside the gap, `V(z) = Σ_y (z − y)⁻²`. **Must:** the pair quantity is an honest pointwise value of a field smooth across the gap and carrying none of the pair's singularity, so any bound on the regular field over the gap interval bounds `Δ`. **Cannot:** object that the adverse diffusion of the raw field near a close pair obstructs this — that adversity belongs to the pair's own singular part, which this identity removes.
- MUST-447 — the pair field is bounded by the one-sided fields at the two zeros. Each term of the regular field is monotone across the gap in the direction of its own zero, so the left-hand terms are largest at the left endpoint and the right-hand terms at the right endpoint. With the pair field a pointwise value of the regular field inside the gap, this gives `Δ ≤ Σ_{y < x₁} (x₁ − y)⁻² + Σ_{y > x₂} (x₂ − y)⁻²` without locating the interior point. **Must:** neither sum carries a near-singular term, the closest contribution on each side lying at the distance of the neighbouring gap, so the pair field of a close pair is controlled by ordinary one-sided spacing data at the two zeros and the pair's own singularity is absent by construction.
- MUST-448 — the virial of the pair force is a pure pair count. For distinct reals `Σ_j Σ_{k≠j} (x_j − x_k)⁻¹ = 0` and `Σ_j x_j Σ_{k≠j} (x_j − x_k)⁻¹ = N(N−1)/2`. Both follow from the swap involution on ordered pairs: the first integrand is antisymmetric, and `x_j/(x_j − x_k) + x_k/(x_k − x_j) = 1` on every unordered pair. **Must:** the virial of the pair force sees only the number of pairs and never the configuration — the first quantity in this lane that is at once exact, unconditional and configuration-blind, hence height-uniform with no spacing input. It is translation invariant: the same value holds about every centre, because the forces balance.
- MUST-449 — the second moment collapses at a configuration-independent rate. Along `ẋ_j = −2 Σ_{k≠j} (x_j − x_k)⁻¹` the virial identity gives `d/dτ Σ_j (x_j − c)² = −2N(N−1)` exactly, hence `Σ_j (x_j(τ) − c)² = Σ_j (x_j(0) − c)² − 2N(N−1)τ` for as long as the flow exists. Nonnegativity of the second moment forces `τ ≤ Σ_j (x_j(0) − c)² / (2N(N−1))`, so some pair collides. **Must:** a collision criterion exists that requires no bound on any field, no selection of a favourable pair and no spacing hypothesis. The open core of MUST-443…447 is bypassed rather than solved.
- MUST-450 — the signed observables of the flow are exactly the convex ones. Symmetrisation with a weight `g` gives `2 Σ_j g(x_j) F_j = Σ_{j≠k} [g(x_j) − g(x_k)]/(x_j − x_k)`, the off-diagonal sum of the divided differences of `g`. **Must:** `Σ_j G(x_j)` is nonincreasing along the flow exactly when `g = G′` is monotone, i.e. exactly when `G` is convex. `G = (x − c)²` is the unique case in which every divided difference is the same constant `2`, which is the source of its configuration-blind rate.
- CANNOT-451 — the virial does not localise. A convex function on `ℝ` bounded above is constant. **Cannot** therefore localise any signed observable of the flow to a window: a localised virial weight would be a nonconstant convex function bounded above. **Must** instead carry the external field explicitly. Centring on the window's own centroid cancels the leading external effect exactly — `Σ_j u_j/(d − u_j) = Σ_j u_j²/(d(d − u_j))` whenever `Σ_j u_j = 0` — and the residual has a fixed sign, since `d(d − u_j) ≥ |d|(|d| − r) > 0` for `|u_j| ≤ r < |d|`: the outside zeros always oppose collapse, by at most `4 I₂ Σ_y [|d_y|(|d_y| − r)]⁻¹`. **Cannot** conclude collapse for a unit window of the zeta zeros from that bound alone: with `N ≍ ϱ` the internal rate is `≍ N²` while the external sum diverges logarithmically as the nearest outside zero approaches the window edge, giving `≍ N² log N`. The sharp remaining gap is the boundary divergence of `Σ_y |d_y|⁻²`, not the pair field of MUST-447.
- MUST-452 — the block collapse threshold is one formula, `δ²E < 6/(N+1)`. A block of `N` evenly spaced zeros with gap `δ` has second moment `N(N² − 1)δ²/12` about its centroid, so the virial criterion `4 I₂ E < 2N(N−1)` is exactly `(N + 1)·δ²E < 6`, with `E` the field of the zeros outside the block. **Must:** the whole family of collision criteria is a single law with a single constant; `N = 2` returns the pair criterion `δ²E < 2`. With every outside gap at least `G`, the telescoping bound `Σ_{m≥1} (mG)⁻² ≤ 2/G²` gives `E ≤ 4/G²` and hence the self-contained threshold `δ/G < (3/(2(N + 1)))^{1/2}`, which at `N = 2` is `δ/G < 1/√2`.
- CANNOT-453 — enlarging the block is not a route around the pair field. `I₂/(N(N−1)) = (N + 1)δ²/12`: the moment of inertia outgrows the pair count by exactly one power of `N`. **Cannot** therefore extract anything from the `N²` internal collapse rate — the threshold `6/(N + 1)` is strictly decreasing, so every block larger than a pair is strictly harder to collapse and the pair is optimal. **Must** keep the criterion at `N = 2` and attack the pair field itself. This closes the window-enlargement successor of CANNOT-451 at its source: the earlier diagnosis that the obstruction was the logarithmic boundary divergence of `Σ_y |d_y|⁻²` was wrong, since the criterion degrades like `1/(N + 1)` even with a perfect buffer.
- MUST-454 — which observables have a sign is a two-point question. For an odd pair kernel `F` the rate of `Σ_j G(x_j)` along `ẋ_j = Σ_{k≠j} F(x_j − x_k)` is `(1/2) Σ_{j≠k} (g(x_j) − g(x_k))·F(x_j − x_k)` with `g = G′`, and a two-point configuration evaluates to exactly `(g p − g q)·F(p − q)`. **Must:** the rate is nonpositive for every finite configuration precisely when `(g x − g y)·F(x − y) ≤ 0` for all `x, y`. The classification of signed observables is independent of the configuration and of the number of zeros.
- CANNOT-455 — no pair dynamics has a bounded nonconstant Lyapunov observable. Dichotomy on the kernel. If `F < 0` throughout `(0,∞)`, the two-point condition says exactly that `g` is monotone, i.e. that `G` is convex, and a convex function bounded above on `ℝ` is constant. If `F` is negative on one interval of `(0,∞)` and positive on another, then `g` is nondecreasing under every shift in the first and nonincreasing under every shift in the second; a sign interval `(α, β)` propagates to every shift beyond `αβ/(β − α)`, since `t/k` lands in `(α, β)` for an integer `k`, so `g` is both nondecreasing and nonincreasing under all large shifts and is constant, `G` is affine and `Σ_j G(x_j)` is conserved. **Cannot** therefore obtain a localised monotone observable from any pairwise zero dynamics, attractive or not. **Must:** the obstruction of CANNOT-451 is not a property of the kernel `1/u` but of the two-point sign condition; it needs no irrationality and no regularity of `g`, and covers every pair interaction at once.
- CANNOT-456 — the backward-collision criterion has no direction that proves RH. Reality is preserved forward in heat time, so `H_0` has only real zeros exactly when `Λ ≤ 0`, and `Λ ≥ 0` is known; RH is `Λ = 0`. A collision at some `t < 0` is consistent with `Λ = 0` and with `Λ > 0` alike, so no collision statement separates them. **Cannot** therefore reach RH from the criterion in any form. **Must:** its only consumer is the contrapositive — finitely many non-real zeros of `H_t` for a single `t < 0` refutes RH — whose antecedent is strictly stronger than the Ki–Kim–Lee finiteness available for `t > 0`. The surviving content of the lane is MUST-448…452 and CANNOT-451/453/455, which are laws of pairwise zero dynamics rather than of the critical line.
- MUST-457 — the far field of the zero divisor costs exactly one log. Unconditionally, for `t ≥ 4` and any finite set of zero copies whose ordinates lie at distance at least `1` from `t`, `Σ (t − γ)⁻² ≤ 160 log(180(t + 1)) + 2K₀ + 960`. The unit-window count is the only input: split by `k = ⌊|t − γ|⌋`, apply the annulus bound `#{k ≤ |t − γ| < k + 1} ≤ 80 log(180(t + k + 1)) + K₀` against the weight `k⁻²`, and use `log(180(t + k + 1)) ≤ log(180(t + 1)) + log(k + 1)` with the two finite sums `Σ_{k≤K} k⁻² ≤ 2` and `Σ_{k≤K} log(k + 1)·k⁻² ≤ 12`. **Must:** the second-order field away from a unit neighbourhood costs one log, exactly as the classical first-order bound for `Σ (1/4 + (t − γ)²)⁻¹` does. **Cannot** therefore locate the obstruction of MUST-447 in the tail of the one-sided fields: the tail is `O(log t)` unconditionally, and the entire difficulty is the zeros within distance `1` of the pair.
- CANNOT-458 — no explicit-formula realization carries the collision quantum. The two-prime carrier summand `χ_p(ρ)χ_p(1−ρ̄) = χ_p(ρ)·conj χ_p(1−ρ)` is holomorphic × antiholomorphic at one point; every holomorphic clock `λ` with `|λ| = 1` on the critical line satisfies `|λ(1−s̄)|·|λ(s)| = 1`, so each free orbit contributes exactly one exponentially growing mode. [L] The reflected two-prime excess tends to `K(T) ∈ 2ℕ`, while the holomorphic one (the clock of `lag_explicit_formula` at `X = 4^a9^b`) is eventually bounded iff `K(T) = 0` and unbounded otherwise (`XiHolomorphicTwoPrimePencilGrowthDichotomy:xiTwoPrimePencil_reflected_holomorphic_exchange`). **Cannot** therefore transport the sub-quantum margin (`<2`) of any HC form to the prime side: source-side the margin is infinite and the whole difficulty is finiteness at the `√X` lag scale (CANNOT-326). Quantized HC forms are zero-indexed; source-realizable ones are growth criteria. [L] Classification for every entire clock `Λ` with `|Λ| = 1` on the line (`XiLineUnimodularClockExcessCriterion`): `Λ(s)·conj Λ(1−s̄) = 1`; the window excess is unbounded iff some zero has `|Λ(ρ)| ≠ 1`, and otherwise converges to the aliasing charge, which is `≥ 2` per off-line zero — the quantum survives only on the unimodular set `{|Λ| = 1}`, which for prime clocks `p^{s−½}` is the critical line.
- MUST-459 — an ordinate collision is not a coincidence. Under `F(1−s̄) = conj F(s)` one off-line zero forces its partner at the same ordinate with no extra equation, and such collisions persist under locally uniform symmetric perturbation (Hurwitz; [L] `XiHolomorphicTwoPrimePencilGrowthDichotomy:eventually_exists_horizontalCollision_of_tendstoLocallyUniformly`). **Must** treat collision exclusion as exclusion of one off-line zero: it has codimension zero in the symmetric class, so no transversality, genericity or Sard argument applies (corrects the framing of CANNOT-402).

- CANNOT-464 — no closure of the single-base (dyadic) lag gap from exact-progression or prime-2 inputs. Bounded `a_{2^m}` leaves only the non-attained regime, whose counterexample is a sparse approximate comb of off-line zeros at ordinates `≈ γ₀ + 2πk/log 2` above every off-line zero (shadow law). Putnam/van Frankenhuijsen exclude only exact consecutive vertical progressions; approximate sparse dyadic combs are generic on the critical line (`γ log 2/2π` equidistributed mod 1); the Bohr mean `(1−2^{−s₀})^{−1}` along `s₀ + 2πik/log 2` excludes only positive-density exact combs; Landau's `Σ_{γ≤T} 2^{kρ}` is carried by on-line zeros (off-line count `o(T)`); prime-2 self-similarity is shared by Helson series with off-line zeros (KR-R2). **Cannot** decide `sup_m a_{2^m} < ∞ ⇒ RH` without arithmetic information on density-zero sets of off-line zeros; the physical FZZ-P source adds none (its Mellin completeness is Nyman–Beurling for the Fermi/Bose kernel modulo the explicit Blaschke factor at `1 + 2πik/log 2`).

- CANNOT-466 — no unconditional witness rigidity above Mellin exponent 0.
